  "long_description": [

      "en": "In the traditions of the New Zealand Māori, Hakawai (also known as Hōkioi in the North Island) was a mythological bird sometimes heard in the night sky but rarely seen. It was held to be one of eleven tapu sacred birds belonging to Raka-maomao, the wind deity.",

        "hakawai-mythology-c02"

    },
    {
      "id": "Burung ini tinggal di langit dan hanya turun ke bumi saat malam hari, dengan suara pekikan khas yang disusul oleh deru kencang laksana benda melaju kencang di udara. Mendengar suara Hakawai secara tradisional dianggap sebagai pertanda buruk yang meramalkan pecahnya peperangan.",
      "en": "Inhabiting the heavens, it descended to earth only at night, producing a vocal call followed by an aerial roar as of an object moving at high speed. Hearing the Hakawai was traditionally viewed as an ominous portent presaging war.",

        "id": "Ipilja-ipilja hidup bersama keluarganya di rawa Numarika di muara Angoroko River di Groote Eylandt.",
        "en": "Ipilja-ipilja lives with his family in the Numarika swamp on Groote Eylandt."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ipilja-ipilja-c03",
      "source_id": "ipilja-ipilja-s1",
      "quote": "The geckos are all \"highly colored,\" with the ipilja-ipilja being about a hundred feet long and adorned with long hair and whiskers.",
      "locator": "Section 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tokek ini memiliki panjang sekitar seratus kaki dengan warna mencolok serta rambut dan kumis panjang.",
        "en": "The gecko is about a hundred feet long, highly colored, with long hair and whiskers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ipilja-ipilja-c04",
      "source_id": "ipilja-ipilja-s1",
      "quote": "At the beginning of the monsoon season, the ipilja-ipilja eats the grasses on the edge of the swamp and drinks a large quantity of the swamp water. He then spews the mixture into the sky.",
      "locator": "Section 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada awal musim monsun, ia memakan rumput dan meminum air rawa lalu menyemburkannya ke langit.",
        "en": "At the start of the monsoon season, he eats grass, drinks swamp water, and spews it into the sky."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ipilja-ipilja-c05",
      "source_id": "ipilja-ipilja-s1",
      "quote": "The water turns into clouds, and the grass binds the clouds together. The ipilja-ipilja, pleased with his efforts, lets out a roar that becomes thunder.",
      "locator": "Section 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Air berubah menjadi awan dan rumput mengikatnya, lalu auman Ipilja-ipilja menjadi petir.",
        "en": "The water becomes clouds bound by grass, and his roar becomes thunder."
      }
    }
  ],

      "en": "In Australian Aboriginal lore from South Australia, Chinny-kinik was a dreaded cannibal giant who terrorized the Yenpulla plain east of the Murray River [chinny-kinik-c01].
